Select a chain, also known as a chain of curves or a contour, to select it for another
command.
Click and hold the required contour with the left mouse button. All sections of the chain that
can be captured are highlighted 1 	. Gaps in the contour are identified. Click with the left
mouse button to apply the selection.

a complete contour line despite its gaps C 	. No gaps are closed. This needs to be taken into
account in the actions that follow.

1
B C

Total entities: The number of selected entities is displayed.
Modes
Influences the path of the chain.
Stop at cross: The chosen contour is continuously selected until the contour line branches
A 	.
The option can be accessed via the context menu Select → Chain. Assign a shortcut in
Shortcuts in More commands. Incorporate the command into a toolbar in Toolbars and tabs
under Commands.
Minimal angle: The chosen contour is continuously selected B 	. Selecting the contour at a
bifurcation ignores the minimal angle between the sections for the remainder of its course.
If the angles at the bifurcation between the sections are equal, automatic selection takes
place based on the entity ID order.
Tangent: Select a contour whose sections come together as being tangential to each other
D 	. Use the Angular tolerance option to specify an angle for a permissible deviation from the
tangency 2 	.
D 1
2
Shortest way: The chosen contour is continuously selected. At a bifurcation, the further
course along solid edges is calculated so that the shortest route is selected.
Select
User driven: Automatically select a chain from a start to an end entity and influence the path
E 	. The Modes influence the path of the chain.
Steps:
1. Click the Curves button. Curve selection is greyed out if User driven is not selected.
2. Click the start entity with the left mouse button 3 	. The click position affects the initial
search direction. The arrow always points towards the other end of the selected entity
4 	. The entity remains selected.
3. Change the direction of the arrow by double-clicking, if necessary.
4. Click the end entity with the left mouse button 5 	. The entity remains selected.
5. The recognized chain is highlighted. The start and end are marked with a temporary
point.
6. It is possible to adjust the course of the chain between the start and end entities 6 	.
To do so, go to the exact position where the unwanted branch of the chain occurs, and
click the desired branch with the left mouse button. Holding down the CTRL key at the
same time allows you to remove a selected entity from the chain.
7. It is possible to save the selected chain using the Save selection command on the
Curves context menu.
For a closed chain, click an entity directly next to the start entity to make it the end entity. In
a closed chain, the temporary points are no longer displayed.
CAD

Snap entities Snapping is used for precise ‘latching’ to the geometrically significant position of an entity.
Snapping is subject to these rules:
• A smaller entity has a greater priority than a larger entity. Points are found first, followed
by curves and then faces.
• The snap priority of points (end point, middle point and so on) can be defined by the user.
• When snapping with arc and circle, the following applies: If the cursor approaches the
curve from the inside, the middle point is always found. If the cursor comes from “out-
side”, the arc or circle is preselected as an entity.
Controlling the snap behavior:
None: Disable snapping. A position can be generated anywhere.
CAD
157

-- 171 of 622 --

Snap only: Latch only to positions permitted by selected snap types in the snap
selection filter.
Automatic: If the cursor is located near a snap point of a permitted snap point
type of the snap selection filter, it is selected. Otherwise, a position can be
created anywhere.
By selecting a snap point type directly, latch only to a position permitted by this snap point
type.

Shows middle point
Shows center

Lists the attributes of one or more entities. The information that is available adapts itself to
the type composition of the selected entities. When one entity is selected, all the properties
are made available. When multiple entities are selected, the intersecting set of useful
parameters of the simultaneously selected entities is calculated, such as the size in the X, Y
and Z direction. If no information can be determined in such a case, this is indicated by the
text ‘Varying ...’. Entities can be optionally added to or removed from the selection.

the CTRL key.
All changeable properties are highlighted by a different background color.

Application
Example 5. Analysis of swarf cutting with barrel cutters
Other analysis options such as Analysis → Face spherical are designed for spherical (tool)
geometries. When the curvatures are analyzed, no direction information is displayed. This is
also not necessary here as the same radius exists for each direction of the sphere.
This type of analysis is not really suitable for barrel cutters, for example, in the case of
low-angle swarf cutting, as two radii of the tool with significantly different sizes are to be
considered.
For selectable positions, it is possible to determine whether collision-free machining is
possible for the following parameters:
• Barrel radius
• Machining direction
• Lead angle
The following procedure may be suggested for an analysis:
1. For the analysis, enable the display of Principal radius 1 and Principal radius 2.
2. Display the global minimum radius. A position is generated.
3. Snap this position with the analysis tripod.
4. Set the Angle according to the planned machining direction, for example, set the value
0° for machining along the U or V isoparametric line.
5. Check whether the displayed radii suit the planned tool. Check whether a more suitable
radius (of another tool) could result in better machining. If a radius that is too small is
chosen, the benefits offered by machining with a barrel cutter will be lost. If a radius
CAD
170

-- 184 of 622 --

that is too large is chosen, this will provoke a collision between the tool and the contour
to be machined.
6. Change the angle to find a direction for the inclination of the tool (lead angle) that
provides a radius that suits the planned tool.
7. Alternatively, change the planned machining direction. In other words, instead of ma-
chining in the direction of an isoparametric line, machine in another direction in which
the radius of the tool can ‘nestle’ against the contour to be machined without any
danger of collision.

Action on target entities
Delete: Delete duplicates.
Hide: Hide duplicates.
Tag: Identify duplicates with tag from Tag name.
Select: Select duplicates.
Tolerance
Tolerance controls the deviation from which (seemingly) identical entities are classified as
different entities.
Tag name
Identify duplicates with Tag from Tag name.
Keep
Analytical before NURBS: Retain the ‘analytical’ entity or preferably deal with (for example,
delete) NURBS duplicates.
First created: Keep the entity that was created first.
Last modified: Keep the entity that was created last.
The three rules are given an order of priority – Analytical before NURBS has the highest
priority. The other two rules follow, each with a lower priority.
Target entities
Select the entities that are intended for Action on target entities. Duplicates deletes all
duplicates, for example. Use Basic entities to process the entities that have not been
identified as duplicates. The number of selected entities is displayed.
List
The list shows the analysis results as a list of all basic entities and their duplicates on the
basis of their entity ID.
Check quality / healing
Check the quality of entities and repair specific data.
Analysis → Check quality / healing
Check the geometry of curves, faces and the solid topology, for example:
• Differences between the tolerance of the face and its boundary
• Differences in the tolerance from corners and boundaries of faces, i.e. a ‘vertex-edge
gap’
• Incorrect edge sequence
• Gaps between faces (non-manifold) for checking whether a solid is closed and whether
the volume is therefore fully delineated.
• Self-cuts for boundaries and edges
• Loops in boundaries and edges
• Entities smaller than tolerance, for example, faces whose area is smaller than the value
resulting from the square of the tolerance.
• Irregular control points of curves
• Irregular parametrization of faces and curves

-- 197 of 622 --

Curvature type
Select a curvature type for the analysis 2 	.
The curvature is the inverse of the radius. If a radius remains constant, the curvature is
also constant. If the curvature converges to 0, the radius is ‘infinite’. If the radius converges
to 0, a very large curvature exists. The two principal curvatures are the minimum and the
maximum curvature values from all curvatures at the analyzed point.
Mean: Displays the ‘mean curvature’ A 	.
The mean curvature is the average of the two principal curvatures (k 1 and k 2) for a point
analyzed on a face.
Gaussian: Displays the ‘Gaussian curvature’ B 	.
The Gaussian curvature (K) is the product of the two principal curvatures (k 1 and k 2) for a
point analyzed on a face.
• K = 0 shows where the face is planar or single-curved. The face is planar, cylindrical or
spherical at the analyzed point.
• K > 0 shows where the principal curvatures have the same sign. The face is bowl-shaped
at the analyzed point.
• K < 0 shows where the principal curvatures have opposite signs. The face is saddle-sha-
ped at the analyzed point.
Min. radius/Min. diameter: Find areas with the smallest radius/diameter C 	. The values are
signed. In an analysis area that is not restricted, the smallest values tend to be in the middle
of the analysis area.
Abs. min. radius / Abs. min. diameter: Find the absolute values of the smallest radii/diame-
ters D 	. The smallest values are located at the left edge of the analysis area.
A B D
2

them with a separate color 6 	. Select the color.

Sphere size
Size: Enter a radius or diameter for the analysis B 	.
Two areas can be determined as the analysis result.
Greater than: The area can be reached using a sphere, in other words, it can potentiality be
machined using a tool with this diameter 7 	. Select the color for marking the entities.

Check collisions: A collision check of the faces involved with a spherical shape in order to
identify the areas for the correct tool diameter. Conversely, the collision check also makes
it possible to detect sharp edges and corners, for whose production an electrode may
be necessary. Without collision checking, each individual face is analyzed for its inherent
minimum curvature/radius. With collision checking, all adjacent faces are additionally taken
into account for the diameter/radius.

On view
Aligns a workplane to the current view.
Workplane → On view
Aligns a workplane to the current view. The view plane lies parallel to the screen and runs
through the origin of the previous workplane. The world workplane is not changed.
Around X
Rotate the workplane around the X axis.
Workplane → Rotate → Around X
Rotate the workplane around the X axis. It is always the new workplane that has not yet
been saved that is rotated.
Around Y
Rotate the workplane around the Y axis.
Workplane → Rotate → Around Y
Rotate the workplane around the Y axis. It is always the new workplane that has not yet
been saved that is rotated.
Around Z
Rotate the workplane around the Z axis.
Workplane → Rotate → Around Z
Rotate the workplane around the Z axis. It is always the new workplane that has not yet
been saved that is rotated.
Move
Rotate and move a workplane.
Workplane → Move
Rotate and move a workplane. Double-click an axis designation with the left mouse button
to reverse the direction of the axis. The current values can be followed in the dialog.
Motion
You select a new origin for the workplane using a point. You can also specify the position
by entering the X, Y and Z coordinates of the new origin ( Delta). Enter an angle ( Angle) to
rotate the workplane around the individual axes.
Entering an origin with Point disables input as delta values.
The new position can also be selected interactively using the manipulator. To move the
origin or an axis, or to rotate an 'arc', grab the manipulator with the left mouse button.

Save as is used to save the workplane in the document. Click the line with the left mouse
button and enter a name. You can also select an existing workplane to assign the new
position to this workplane and thus overwrite its existing position. To do this, click the right

Redefine as
Adjust the definition type of an existing workplane to design it differently
with other options and values.
Context menu → Redefine as → ...
“Restarts” definition of a selected workplane. If, for example, a workplane that was created
with the On face command is selected, it can be changed into a workplane defined with the
options and values of the On curve command. Or select a free non-associative workplane
to change it into an associative workplane on a face.
Select an existing saved workplane. Click the right mouse button to open the context menu,
select Redefine as and select a command to redefine the workplane. Help information
on this step is available in the description of the selected command. The name of the
workplane is reset. If you want to keep the same name as before, it must be selected from
the name list again.
In contrast, modifying an entity with the Edit → Change parameters command maintains the
current definition and allows you to modify the current properties.
Set current
Set a workplane to be the current workplane.
Workplane → Set current
Set a workplane to be the current workplane. Select the workplane. The workplane is set to
be the current workplane and displayed in color. The previously used workplane is switched
to inactive and displayed in grey.
A workplane can also be set to be the current workplane
• via the context menu on the workplane tab,
• directly in the Graphics area by double-clicking the axes of an inactive workplane with the
left mouse button or
• by selecting and calling the command on the context menu
.

Copy to clipboard.
Edit → Copy
Copy entities to the clipboard.
Control the behavior for copying
The settings from the copy_defaults.xml file are used when copying entities from document
to document via the clipboard.
The file with the general preferences is in C:\Program Files\OPEN MIND\hyperCAD-S\
[version]\files\factorysettings. For individual presets copy this file in the folder C:\Users\
[USER]\AppData\Roaming\OPEN MIND\hyperCAD-S\[version]\commands\defaults. If the
folder does not exist, create the folder. Customize the settings in the copied file.
The file copy_defaults.xml has the following structure:
<?xml version="1.0"?>
<PBag name="filecmd.Copy"
version="1">
<property name="SaveAllMaterials"
type="omslLong"
uuid="40dff002-f0e9-59f9-b8a8-04776d96aea9"
dimension="Dimensionless">1</property>
<property name="SaveMating"
type="omslLong"
uuid="40dff002-f0e9-59f9-b8a8-04776d96aea9"
dimension="Dimensionless">0</property>
<property name="SaveSpreadsheet"
type="omslLong"
uuid="40dff002-f0e9-59f9-b8a8-04776d96aea9"
dimension="Dimensionless">0</property>
<property name="UseCurrentWP"
type="omslLong"
uuid="40dff002-f0e9-59f9-b8a8-04776d96aea9"
dimension="Dimensionless">1</property>
</PBag>
Attribute Elements or property Configuration possibilities
SaveAllMaterials Copy material. 0: Do not copy
1: Copy	SaveMating Copy mating
SaveSpreadsheet Copy variables.
UseCurrentWP Copy current workplane
The attributes uuid contained in the XML structure are the same everywhere. Every user
can use the file.

Paste
Paste from clipboard.

Paste from the clipboard into the same and into other open*.hmc documents.
Control paste behavior
The settings from the paste_defaults.xml file are used when pasting elements via the clip-
board from document to document. Also, these settings are used for dragging a document
into an open document with the CTRL key pressed.
The file with the general preferences is in C:\Program Files\OPEN MIND\hyperCAD-S\
[version]\files\factorysettings. For individual presets copy this file in the folder C:\Users\
[USER]\AppData\Roaming\OPEN MIND\hyperCAD-S\[version]\commands\defaults. If the
folder does not exist, create the folder. Customize the settings in the copied file.

<?xml version="1.0"?>

Undo
Undo an action.
Edit → Undo
You can Undo an unlimited number of actions. Right-click the icon to open a list of recent
actions. Click an action in the list with the left mouse button to undo all the actions down to
and including the action.
Redo
Redo an action.
Edit → Redo
You can redo an unlimited number of actions. To restore the actions again, use Redo.
Right-click the icon to open a list of recent actions. Click an action in the list with the left
mouse button to redo all the actions down to and including the action.
Spreadsheet
Manage and calculate variables.
Edit → Parametric → Spreadsheet
Import variables, manage them centrally, assign values to them in order to enter variables
instead of values in dialogs. Use formulas to calculate values.
Main menu
Ok: Confirm your entry.
Del.: Delete your entry.
Func.: Select a variable, formula, command or constant.
Move up: Move a row in the table upwards. First click the row to select it.
Move down: Move a row in the table downwards. First click the row to select it.
Append: Append an additional row to the bottom of the table.

There are three columns (B, C and D) in the Excel file with values for the variables. Of

ranges can be configured in the same file, for example.

Dialog: Configure import
Configure which information should be read from which cells.
File name: The path and file name of the selected Excel file are displayed. Use ... to open a

Rows
Enter from which row (Starting) to which row (Ending) the information is located. If Ending is
equal to 0, all rows of the file are read.
Columns
For Name of a variable, Value, Data type, Physical quantity and Unit of measure of the
parameter list, enter from which column of the file this information should be read.
Default settings
Select Data type, Physical quantity and Unit of measure as defaults in case the information
cannot be read from the file because the specified cell is missing or empty.
Example 7. Ignore type specification from Excel file
If, for example, a value of ‘0 / Ignore’ is specified for the type for the column address,
this means that all imported variables should be set to the value specified under Default
settings, Data type (for example, ‘Double’).
Show a preview for checking purposes. The preview can be accessed via the magnifying
glass in the upper part of the dialog. display of warnings and errors with the help of symbols
– for example, when importing into already existing data, if variables differ only in their
spelling or are the same but the data type differs, for example.
Import data once
Use Import file to import data from an Excel file once. No link is generated to this file.
Select the Microsoft Excel file format *.xlsx, *.xls or *.csv in the file dialog. Then select the
file and Open.
In the dialog that follows, configure which information should be read from which cells.
Transfer data permanently
Use Manage link to permanently transfer data from several Excel files and manage links to
these files.
The linked Excel files are monitored if the *.hmc document is open in which the Excel files
are linked. Information is displayed when an Excel file has been saved.
A link to an Excel file consists of the following:
• Link name: Enter a freely selectable name for the link.
• On / off: Select a state for the activation. If the specified Excel file with the associated
configuration is active, it means that the data will be read in again when updating.
• Imported file and Path: File directory and file name of the Excel file are displayed.
+ : Add a link to a file or a data folder. Proceed in the same way as for Import file.
- : Remove the link to the selected file.
Clear all: Remove all links to files.
Update selection: Update data of the selected files.
Update all: Update all existing data.
Modify: For the selected file, change the settings for transferring the data. Proceed in the
same way as for Import file.
Update link
Update imported data of permanently linked Excel files.
CAD

NOTE
A spreadsheet with an external link cannot be updated or changed if the
Excel file is open. Close the Excel file or release the Excel workbook for
collaboration. The Excel file must be opened via the share link. Only then
can collaboration take place during processing.

ground is displayed in red.

read only. If the name of such a variable is changed, the link to the Excel file is removed
and the variable is changed to a “local” variable.

Overview of the structure of the parameter list
The parameter list is set up as a table.
To edit a cell, click the cell with the left mouse button.
Multiple rows can be selected with the left mouse button while pressing the CTRL and/or
SHIFT key at the same time.
If the name of a variable is changed, the name will also change in an assigned dimension
and in the formula used.
Numerical values in the table cells are shown in blue.
Table 67. Content of the parameter list
Column Purpose
Variable Enter a name for the variable.
Formula / value Enter a numerical value or a formula.
Result The value entered or the value calculated in the formula is dis-
played.
CAD
222

-- 236 of 622 --

Column Purpose
Physical quantity Select a physical quantity.
Select the quantity 'Integer' to enter a number of copies or e.g.
offsets.
Unit of measure Select a unit of measure. The selection you make here is predeter-
mined by the physical quantity.
Data type Select a data type for the variable.
• Boolean value (Bool): A yes/no condition, where 'Bool' is the
data type used in the software.
• Integer value (Integer): Numerical value without decimal point,
where 'Integer' is the data type used in the software.
• Decimal value (Double): Numerical value with a decimal point,
where 'Double' is the data type used in the software.
Min. Specify the minimum lower value.
Max. Specify the maximum upper value.
Used Specifies how often the variable is integrated in a formula.
Assigned Number of times the variable is assigned in other commands.
Description Enter explanations to make it easy to understand the row later on.
Depending on the selected context, select the mathematical functions, mathematical con-
stants and defined variables on the context menu or call the following commands:
Append row: Append an additional row to the bottom of the table.
Insert row: Insert an additional row above the selected row.
Delete row: Delete the selected row. When you delete a row, a reference error may occur if
the defined variable is used again. In this case the field is highlighted in red and the formula
needs to be revised.
List variables depending on it: List all the variables that are dependent on the selected
variable.
Show assigned entities: Highlight entities in which a variable has been assigned.
List used variables: List all variables that are reused in the parameter list.
Evaluate variable: Force the formula to be evaluated.
Evaluate spreadsheet: Force all formulas to be evaluated. The spreadsheet is updated in
real time. Not only is there an immediate recalculation; the syntax is also checked. The
main purpose is to (re)check for division by zero or negative square roots and so on.
Variables: List all variables that are defined in the parameter list.
Functions: List all the available mathematical functions.
Constants: List all the available constants.
Filter variables
Filter the display of the rows in the parameter list.
CAD
223

-- 237 of 622 --

Show exported variables: Display all rows in which a variable is exported, in other words,
that is to be used for CAM programming.
Show assigned variables: Display all rows where a variable is defined that uses a formula to
access other variables.
Show dependent variables: Display all rows with variables that are used in commands, such
as for dimensions.
Show independent variables: Display all rows where a variable is defined that is not used
any further.
Show automatically generated variables: Show all rows with formulas entered in com-
mands.

Defining variables
Variable names may consist of letters of the Roman alphabet and Arabic numerals. A
variable must always begin with a letter. A distinction is made between upper and lower
CAD
224

-- 238 of 622 --

case. Space characters, tabs and line breaks may not be used in variables and formulas.
Special characters, such as ()[]{}.,<>;'~*+-=/\\\"^!?#, are also forbidden in variable names.
Mathematical operators and conditions
All JScript instructions, such as if...else, may be used if they make sense in a parameter list.
Table 69. Examples of operations
a = 2 a is equal to 2 (assignment)
a == a a is equal to a (comparison)
b != a b is not equal to a (comparison)
a * b a times b
a / b a divided by b
a % b Find remainder of division of a divided by b
a + b a plus b
a - b a minus b
a & b bitwise a AND b
a | b bitwise a OR b
a ^ b bitwise a XOR b
Table 70. Bit operation
a << b Shift a b bits to the left (fill with zeros)
a >> b Shift a b bits to the right (copy the sign)
a >>> b Shift a b bits to the right (fill with zeros)
IMPORTANT
Please note the following restrictions. Instructions may only comprise
one line. The last instruction must represent a value. The instructions
are only checked for syntax errors, circular references among variables
and exceptions that are triggered by the JScript interpreter. A complex in-
struction, that is, one that consists of a complex sequence of statements,
can easily lead to a crash. Instructions must be formulated carefully and
checked.
Mathematical functions
To enter or modify data, double-click the respective table field. Then enter the function call
or select the function from the context menu.
A function may be preceded by ‘Math.’, but does not have to be. The arguments of the
function must be separated by a comma and the decimal places must be separated by a
decimal point.

Automatically generated variable
If, for example, for the area of a square with an edge length of 50 pow(50,2) is entered in an
option in a command dialog, the result 2500 will be calculated and used.
If, however, an equals sign is placed in front of the formula, for example, =pow(50,2)
the calculated result will be used and, in addition, the formula will be transferred to the
parameter list and a variable will be generated, such as _1.

Using variables in
hyperMILL®
Variables can be selected in the dialogs during CAM programming. To do this, click the
triangle next to the input field and select the variable. The variable and its description from
the spreadsheet are displayed.
• Then, enable the variable for use in CAM programming in theVariable column of the
spreadsheet.
• Show exported variables: Display all rows in which a variable is exported, in other words,
that is to be used for CAM programming.
• If the value of such a variable changes, the
hyperMILL® jobs are marked as changed.
• If the name of a variable used in this way is changed in the spreadsheet, the link between
the spreadsheet and the variable used in the dialog is lost. The background of the input
field in the CAM programming dialog is colored red as a warning.
• The variables used during CAM programming are marked as “Exported” in the Assigned
column of the spreadsheet.
•
hyperMILL® user variables are not listed in the spreadsheet.
TIP
There are different forms of variables in
hyperMILL®.
• Variables from the Spreadsheet. If these variables are to be used
during CAM programming, enable use of them in the spreadsheet.
• User-defined tags to manage user-defined properties and information
that can be linked with entities in the graphics area. Metadata such
as product and manufacturing information (PMI) can be transferred to
TAGs.
•
hyperMILL® user variables in CAM programming
• Script variables within AUTOMATION Center scripts. Values of other
variables can be transferred using AUTOMATION Center commands.
Don’t forget the environment variables in the Windows system settings
and the entries in the Windows registry and don’t forget to distinguish
them from these variables.

Solids In the case of direct modeling, the solid is automatically closed when deleting a

Break
Break down entities into their subentities.
Edit → Break
Groups are broken down by level (non-recursively). Toolpaths are broken down into poly-
lines, vector graphics such as imported text or dimensions are broken down into lines and
polylines and rectangles are broken down into lines. Text that uses a vector font is broken
down into polylines. CAD features are broken down into faces.
Dimensions are broken down into curves. Curve entities are separated from a V-sketch
entity. The V-sketch is deleted.
Parametrically modelled solid patterns are broken into static solids.
Select the entities. Press the ESC key to exit the command.

CAD
237

-- 251 of 622 --

Move and rotate entities, such as meshes created from imported scan data, at the same

Reference systems can also be fitted. They are marked with <Selec-
ted_Ref_Sys_Name>_MOVED.
Select

?
Assign
Select points (pairs) where positions should be matched as far as possible. The number
of target entities must be equal to the number of base entities. An already selected target
point cannot be selected as a start point and vice versa.
A number is displayed next to the two points of a point pair in order to be able to better
identify the points of a pair.

1

Constraints
Reference system
WP: Select a workplane that is essential for locking the translation and fixing rotation axes
4 	. If no selection is made, the world workplane will be used.
Locked translation
X, Y and Z: Select a proportion of the translation that should remain constant when fitting.
Fixed rotation axis
X, Y and Z: Select a proportion of the rotation that is to remain constant when fitting.
To prevent any rotation, it is sufficient to lock two rotation axes, because if two axes cannot
change their direction, the direction of the third axis is also fixed.
Weight
Point: Select a start point and enter a numerical value to determine the ‘intensity’ with which
to match the points of the pair of points. Select further points (pairs) one after the other and
enter a weight. Those with the lowest weight are matched less than those with a higher
weight.
The default value is 1. The weights are ‘relative to each other’. For example, if all points
were weighted with 1000, it would be as if they were all weighted with 1.
Mating
Mate faces, groups and solids and define the relationship between them.
Edit → Mating
Mate faces, groups and solids. Define a mating pair relationship between individual faces,
groups and solids. This should be a stepwise process. Once the two faces have been
selected and the identified relationship is correct, confirm the result by clicking Apply and
create the next mating pair. The mating pairs appear on the Model tab.
The software will automatically attempt to suggest a meaningful relationship for a mating
pair. This depends on the selected entities. As an example, two cylinders will be arranged
to be Concentric. For two planes, for example, Coincident will be assumed automatically.
CAD
239

-- 253 of 622 --

This suggestion can then be changed to Parallel, for example, and a Distance can be
inserted.
To mate multiple faces together, collect the faces into a group. To mate meshes, collect the
meshes and faces together into a group. Place the faces on the mesh in advance so that
they can be used as mating aids for the mesh.
The relationships between multiple solids and groups can be established in any order.
Persistent: Permanently save the relationship defined between a mating pair. Otherwise,
the entities will only be moved and/or rotated.
When moving entities, the relationships saved as mating pairs are observed. If a group or
a solid is moved, all child entities will always move with it. In turn, the movement of these
groups or solids is only possible within the bounds of the constraints defined for the parent
entity. This can mean that a movement executed in the XY direction results in movement
only actually occurring in the X direction, for example. A warning is displayed.
In direct modeling, affected mating pairs are adjusted automatically. The mating continues
to remain in place after direct modeling.
When you use Move / Copy and Paste, the matings are also transferred via the clipboard.
Using File → Save selection, it is possible not to save selected mating pairs or, using File →
Merge, it is possible not to reimport selected mating pairs. Disable the display of matings on
the Model tab in Options → Options / properties in Model structure → Entities to show.
Select
First entity: Select a face of the entity to be moved.
Second entity: Select a second fixed entity. The entity type depends on the mating type. It
is usually a face, but may also be a point or a position, as in the case of the point-plane
constraint.
Always work in a ‘from - to’ sequence. The software will automatically suggest an optimal
relationship for the mating pair. Keep or modify the condition. Always Apply the correct
result before proceeding to the next mating step.
Relationships
The software will attempt to automatically suggest a meaningful relationship for a mating
pair. This depends on the selected entities. The active relationship is highlighted in color. In-
applicable relationships are greyed out. Click to change one of the applicable relationships.

How can a mating pair be changed?
Permanent mating pairs can be modified. To do so, select the entry under Mating in the
model tree on the Model tab and right-click to open the context menu. Then select the
Change parameters command.
You can delete mating pairs. To do so, select the entry under Mating in the model tree on
the Model tab and right-click to open the context menu. Then select the Delete command
and delete using the DEL key.
What happens when a mated entity is modified?
Icon If mated solids or groups are deleted, the mating pairs remain in the model tree.
This is known as ‘dangling’ mating. After inserting another solid or another group,
use Change parameters to assign the missing faces to the dangling mating pairs.
Dangling mating is indicated by an icon in the model tree.
Icon The mating can no longer be used as changing an entity will result in a contradic-
tion. The unused mating is shown by an icon in the model tree.
Mating clamping device
Mating clamping device.
Edit → Mating clamping device
Mate solids or groups as a clamping device. First align the individual solids or groups using
Mating.
NOTE
The command is a specialization of the Mating command. See the de-
scription of this command for more information on the mode of operation.
Persistent: Permanently maintain the defined relationship.

-- 264 of 622 --

default.densities.xml
1
2
Select
Shape: Select entities (solid, mesh, stock model) 1 	. The number of selected entities is

Physical attributes
Mass density: Displays the density of the selected material.
Mass: Displays the weight force based on gravity, which is simplified as the mass, for the
selected entities.
Description: Displays a description that has been stored for the selected material.
Material
Select material from the list 2 	.
User-defined material list
Define a separate material list for workpieces and for eroding with electrodes in the file
default.densities.xml as a default setting in ...\files\materials\.... The modified file, like all
modified files, should be copied to the roaming or company directory and not modified in the
installation directory, for example, in C:\Users\[USER]\AppData\Roaming\OPEN MIND\hy-
perCAD-S\[VERSION]\materials\default.densities.xml.
Materials for workpieces
Enter the name, description and mass density properties of a material in <matter> with the
attributes Name, description and massDensity. Enter the name of the material group in title
within <category>. The unit of measure can be changed globally in <categories> with the
attribute measureUnit and with the values lb/ft³ or kg/dm³. In case of problems, use the
standard escape sequences lb/&amp;#179; or kg/dm&amp;#179. Optionally, also define the
unit of measure for each individual material:
<matter name="0.1234"
description="EN-GJMW-1234"
massDensity="123.4567"
measureUnit="lb/ft³" />
<matter name="0.1234"
description="EN-GJMW-1234"
massDensity="123.4567"
measureUnit="lb/ft&#179;" />
The materials are offered for selection in Edit → Assign material, Electrodes → Modify EDM
parameters and in Electrodes → Print.

Automatic trim
Delete parts of a selected entity up to the next point of intersection with another

Delete parts of a selected entity up to the next point of intersection with another entity.
Select the curve to be automatically trimmed 1 	. The position at which you click influences
the result. The part of the curve that is to be trimmed is highlighted in a preview. If a
boundary exists (curve, face, mesh), the curve will be trimmed to the intersection point. If no
boundary is found, the curve will be deleted completely.
The cursor changes its appearance.
1
Trim curve
Shorten or extend a curve via a boundary.
Modify → Trim curve
If the Curve and Boundary lie on the same plane without an intersection point, an attempt is
made to generate an intersection point for trimming by extending the boundary 2 	.
If the Curve and Boundary are spatially separated from one another so that an intersection
point can also not be generated by extending the boundary, a message is output.
The position at which you click influences the result. The first click position on the first curve
determines which intersection point is used and which section is retained. The second click
position on the second entity determines which section of the second entity is retained.
The Compute on view option trims the curve at the place where its projection on the current
view plane intersects the boundary 4 	.

-- 275 of 622 --

Settings
Use Tolerance to specify the maximum distance for the conversion of the new entities to the
original entities. Use Max. degree to specify the maximum degree for the conversion, Min.
cont. to define the minimum continuity and Max. patches to specify the maximum number of
face patches.
Convert to analytical
Convert NURBS entities into entities that can be described using analytical
geometry.
Modify → Convert to analytical
Faces (for example, NURBS, offset face) are converted into analytical geometry (plane,
cylinder, rotational face). Curves are converted into a line, circular arc or a series of circular
arcs. A series of consecutive circular arc or line segments is generated if a NURBS curve
cannot be converted into a single arc.
Conversion tolerance: Faces are converted within the conversion tolerance. This means
that if, for example, a planar face lies within the specified conversion tolerance, it will
become a plane. Otherwise, the face is not converted. Curves are also converted within the
conversion tolerance.
Angular tolerance: If a series of consecutive circular arc and line segments is generated,
the angular tolerance controls the permissible deviation from the mutual tangency. At an an-
gular tolerance of 0, the number of curve segments increases to approximate the selected
NURBS curve. At a rough angular tolerance of 20, for example, fewer entities are generated
with a lower tangency to one another.
Keep attributes: Color attributes are retained.
Keep original: Specify whether the selected entities should be retained.
Select
Entities: Select curves, faces and faces in solids. If the selected face or curve is already an
analytical entity, it will not be modified. The number of entities is displayed.
Simplify
Simplify faces, boundaries and curves.
Modify → Simplify
Simplify faces and faces within a solid or an entire such solid by combining. The following
are supported:
• Cylinder faces
• Planar faces
• Cone faces
• Rotational faces
• NURBS faces (both faces must have the same control points as the underlying face).
• Linearly extruded faces (Only if the base curves can be simplified. Mostly only for lines
and arcs. Then the linear sweep is a plane or a cylinder.)
Simplify curves, boundaries and edges.
Features are broken up if necessary.
CAD
262

-- 276 of 622 --

NOTE
Overlapping entities cannot be deleted.
Keep original: Specify whether the selected face is to be retained and a copy is to be
created. The selected faces are always replaced with the new face within solids.
Keep attributes: Color attributes are retained.
Tolerance for curves simplification: Enter the permissible maximum size of gaps within all
selected entities for which the entities are to be considered as belonging to each other
when simplifying.
B
A
1
Select

Invert faces UV-parameter
Invert the U and V direction of isoparametric curves in faces.
Modify → Invert faces UV-parameter
Invert the U and V direction of isoparametric curves and so also change the orientation of
faces and faces in solids (open and closed solids). This may be useful, for example, for
Boolean operations, in case of import problems, or for CAM programming on the basis of
face isoparameters.
The direction of the normal vectors of the faces and the U and V direction are shown with
arrows.
The selected entity is converted into NURBS geometry if it is not a planar face or NURBS
face. The default settings of the Convert to NURBS command are used (Degree: C2,
Tolerance: 0.001 mm, Max. degree: 12, Max. patches: 500, Non-rational). A warning and
information about NURBS parameters are output on the Info tab. Depending on the continu-
ity of the initial face, it is not always possible to adhere to the required parameters for the
NURBS approximation.

Faces: Select entities 1 	. The number of selected entities is displayed.

the left mouse button 2 	.

-- 290 of 622 --

Matching curves / boundaries
Use curves and boundaries to choose where the entities to be deformed are to be made to
match the target entities.
Initial
Curves: Select the base entities 3 	. The number of selected entities is displayed.
Target
Curves: Select the target entities of the deformation 4 	. The number of selected entities is
displayed.
For face boundaries, select whether only the Position or the G1 continuity constraint, in
other words, a Tangency must be adhered to.
Invert orientation
Curves: Invert the orientation of an initial or target curve. Select the curve. The number of
selected entities is displayed.
Parameter
Vary the settings if the result does not meet requirements. Information about the precision
that has been achieved is output on the Info tab.
Position: Precision G0 specifies a maximum permissible distance between the target enti-
ties of the deformation and the result.
Tangency: Precision G1 specifies a maximum permissible deviation from the tangency for
the transition to the target entities of the deformation and the result.
Points: Specify the number of points placed on the boundaries that are to be used in
the calculation. Choosing a greater number of points helps to increase precision but also
increases the calculating time.
Rigidity specifies the resistance of the entities that are being deformed to the deformation
for each coordinate axial direction.
NURBS output
Tolerance specifies a positional conversion tolerance for the NURBS entities. Use Max.
degree to specify the maximum degree for the NURBS entities, Min. cont. to define the
minimum continuity and Max. patches to specify the maximum number of face patches.
Example 10. Precise deformation
To achieve a very precise deformation, use the following values as the starting point for the
NURBS output:
• Tolerance: 0.001 mm
• Max. degree: 7
• Min. continuity: 2
• Max. patches: 3600
Example 11. Fast deformation
To achieve a fast calculation in order to gain an initial overview, use the following values as
the starting point for the NURBS output:
• Tolerance: 0.004 mm
• Max. degree: 7
• Min. continuity: 0
CAD
277

• Max. patches: 800

Shape control
Use Thickness 6 	to perform the calculation on the basis of a space. Enter the thickness
of the space or set using the handle. Double-click the handle with the left mouse button to
reverse the direction. Otherwise, the calculation will be performed on the basis of a plane,
where appropriate, such as for 2D geometries and coplanar entities.
Symmetric: The faces for initial reference and target reference in the center of the space
controlled by 'thickness' for the calculation.
Regularize·in·the·middle: Adhere to an additional constraint within the space in order to
ensure the required degree of precision in the middle of the space 7 	. This may be useful if,
for example, the vertical extension of the volumetric deformation is significantly greater than
the extension in the other directions.
Use Density to select the number of constraints (between 1 and 10).
Extension: Enlarge the deformation space greater than the size of the target face.
The Laws that you select specify the direction of the projection onto the target faces and
therefore play a major part in determining the appearance of the volumetric deformation.
• In the direction of the target face normals
• In the piercing direction
• In the direction of the initial face normals
NURBS output
Tolerance specifies a positional conversion tolerance for the NURBS entities. Use Max.
degree to specify the maximum degree for the NURBS entities, Min. cont. to define the
minimum continuity and Max. patches to specify the maximum number of face patches.

Spline
Create or modify a cubic spline.
Curves → Spline
Create or modify a 2D / 3D free-form curve with degree = 3 and continuity = 2 (cubic
spline).
If you select the Closed curve option 3 	, the end point is set automatically to the start point
of the curve, creating a closed curve with a tangential transition. To move points in the Z
axial direction, Use the manipulator. You can use the handles of the manipulator to move
points in the Z direction and in all other directions.
To modify an existing spline curve with a degree of 3 and continuity of 2, select the curve
and then call the Spline command.
Select the Change parameters option on the context menu and modify the spline by drag-
ging the handles. The Modify control points or Convert to NURBS command can be used to
attempt to adjust the degree and continuity.
If the selected spline is planar, it remains on its plane even if the points are moved. A 3D
spline is always modified parallel to the current view. Press the CTRL key to select multiple
spline points at the same time.
To insert an additional interpolation point, click once with the left mouse button on or near
the required spline position.
To delete a control point, select the control point and press SHIFT+DEL or just DEL.

From boundaries
Create a face from a maximum of four boundaries.
Shapes → From boundaries
Create a face from a maximum of four boundaries. The Tolerance defines the permissible
inaccuracy of the boundaries that delineate the edges of the face, so that gaps between
face boundaries or curves are ignored, for example. The tolerance is used to check whether
two curves are connected. If the ends of the curves do not converge within the permissible
tolerance, the face point is placed in the middle between the two ends. The size can be
specified by entering a value or using the slide control. The value range moves between the
'Approximation tolerance' and the hundredfold 'Approximation tolerance'.

From grid
Create a face from several curve chains in one direction plus several
curve chains in the other direction.
Shapes → From grid 2024
Create a face from several curve chains in one direction plus several curve chains in
the other direction A 	. The number of curves per chain can vary. The curve chains in
both directions must intersect within a given tolerance. If curve chains protrude, the last
intersection point is used for the edge boundary. The resulting protruding curve segments
are not included in the face. The face can be created closed in one direction B 	. Both
directions can converge at the start or end of the face, or the face can just consist of three
edge boundaries C 	.
A B C

positions where this situation occurs.

positions where this situation occurs.

already been selected, the curves in the second direction can also be selected via the
curves in the first direction, for example, using a window selection. The curves in the first
direction are not automatically used as curves in the second direction. The number of

settings.
Tension is the target setting for the internal tension of the filling face.
Use No. of points to specify the number of points placed on the boundaries that are to be
used in the calculation during the optimization. The default value for the number of points is
15. If the values entered for the tolerance do not result in the required level of precision, you
can increase the number of points. This will improve the precision at the boundaries.
Iterations specifies the number of calculation loops for the optimization. If the result is not
as precise as required, you can increase the number of loops. Max. degree is the maximum
degree of the filing face. If the result does not meet requirements, the degree can be
decreased. Max. spans specifies the maximum number of NURBS spans of the filling face.

Select
Entities: Select entities 1 	. Selected faces are displayed transparently. The number of
selected entities is displayed.
Entity accuracy
Determine the Entity accuracy of the base entities in their maximum (Max.) and minimum
(Min.) form and display in the graphics area using the Display option. The minimum accura-
cy is shown in green.
Open edges are highlighted in red. The minimum entity accuracy is green, the maximum
entity accuracy is highlighted in blue.
Use the Show inconsistencies option to mark all places where the volume is described
incorrectly. If base entities are outside the tolerance, use the option Separate multishell
solids to select whether a multishell solid or several individual solids are created.
Largest gap
Largest gap: The maximum distance between all merged and all potentially mergeable
edges is displayed. This distance can be used to close a solid that would not be closed if
only the accuracy of the faces and edges is considered.
Two conditions apply to edges that can potentially be merged.
• There must be pairs of open edges that have two vertices in common.
• The edge contours must be compatible in terms of the course of their shape. They must
almost lie ‘parallel next to each other’.
In this way, an attempt is made to only look for edge pairs that make sense.
Example 14. Hole
Potentially mergeable edge pairs such as two half circles, which form a hole, are excluded.
Although they have two vertices in common, they do not almost lie parallel next to each oth-
er. When two common corners are present, there is only a necessary (but not a sufficient)
condition for regarding the two edges as potentially mergeable.
In order to close a solid, the largest gap can be regarded as the still permissible error. As
obviously, solids with real open edges (that is, are not mergeable) cannot be closed even if
the largest gap is assigned as the distance.
CAD
374

-- 388 of 622 --

Set value: Apply the value determined in Largest gap as the value for Assign user defined
tolerance in order to still attain a closed solid.
Assign user defined tolerance: Re(calculate) the solid with the value determined for the
user-defined tolerance. The solid is generated without any gaps.
Open edges
The number of Open edges 4 	is displayed. Create curves 5 	generates curves here.

13. V-sketch
Provide 2D contours, such as milling boundaries or turning contours, with geometric con-
straints. If changes are made to curves within a V-sketch, the V-sketch is updated automat-
ically by means of the dependencies defined via the constraints. Edit the values of the
V-sketch via dimensional constraints and the parameter list.
Overview V-sketch
Create or modify V-sketch.
Drafting → V-sketch → Create / modify
Add automatically defined constraints.
Drafting → V-sketch → Automatic constraints
Add geometric constraints.
Drafting → V-sketch → Geometric constraint
V-sketch dimensioning.
Drafting → V-sketch → Dimensional constraints
Change a measure of a V-sketch.
Drafting → V-sketch → Change dimensional constraint
Hide the highlights for V-sketch constraints.
View → Hide / unhide → Hide V-sketch constraints
Unhide the highlights for V-sketch constraints.
View → Hide / unhide → Show V-sketch constraints
Options for the Sketch and V-sketch commands
File → Options → Sketcher options
There are three types of constraint:
Implicit constraint An implicit constraint is always recognized automatically by
the software. It is not marked with a symbol. An implicit
constraint arises, for example, when a curve end point and
a curve start point match, in other words, both points are
coincident.
Geometric constraint A geometric constraint must be assigned manually. The
V-sketch is adjusted accordingly. The constraints should be
able to be applied in a meaningful way. The software can
search automatically for geometric constraints. Geometric
constraints are marked with symbols. These symbols indi-
cate the type of constraint.
Dimensional constraint The dimensions in a V-sketch. Modifying a measure in a
dimension assigned to entities changes the geometry of the
entity accordingly.
A V-sketch can be created in the following sequence:
CAD
376

-- 390 of 622 --

1. Create the curve entities, for example, using the Drafting → Sketch command.
2. Convert these entities to a V-sketch entity using the Drafting → V-sketch → Create /
modify command. In the model tree on the Model tab, the selected entities are added
to his V-sketch entity.
3. Using the Drafting → V-sketch → Automatic constraints command, geometric con-
straints are determined from the existing position of the entities to each other. This
is also a possible option in the Create / modify command.
4. Using the Drafting → V-sketch → Geometric constraints command, further geometric
constraints between entities are defined. The selected constraint may change the
position of the entities relative to each other.
Dimension a V-sketch to be able to vary it:
• Make sure that the V-sketch entities and their constraints are present in the desired form.
• Call the Drafting → V-sketch → Dimensional constraints command and insert a dimension
for all geometric properties of the entities that are to be modified later on. This is also a
possible option for radii in the Automatic constraints command.
To modify the V-sketch, double-click with the left mouse button on a measure or call the
Drafting → V-sketch → Change dimensional constraint command and select the measure.
Generate a variable optionally under Edit → Spreadsheet. Use this variable as a value for a
dimension. Vary the V-sketch by changing the value of the variable in the parameter list.
The geometry of a V-sketch can be modified subsequently:
• Variant A
• Use a command to modify the geometry, for example, Fillet, to insert a fillet. Permit the
selection on the Selection tab to select an entity within the V-sketch.
• Then call the Create / modify command and select the V-sketch. Gaps and over-de-
fined areas are marked by red points. Make corrections. Click the left mouse button
and press the CTRL key at the same time to separate entities from the V-sketch.
• Variant B
Change the V-sketch interactively. Make sure that the selection within the V-sketch entity
is permitted. Double-click a curve within the V-sketch with the left mouse button. Gener-
ate the required shape of the V-sketch using the preview. Click once with the left mouse
button or press ENTER to confirm the entry.
When the cursor rests on a dimension or a curve, the related entities are highlighted in
color. Make sure that the selection within the V-sketch entity is permitted.
Change the position of a dimension using the Edit → Move text / dimension command.
Remove a constraint within a V-sketch using the Edit → Delete command. Call the com-
mand and select the symbol of the constraint using the left mouse button. Make sure that
the selection within the V-sketch entity is permitted.
Delete a V-sketch using the Edit → Break command. The entities are retained.
Redundancy – if there are two identical symbols on an entity, it can be assumed that a
redundant relationship has been created. To check, allow the selection within the V-sketch
and rest the cursor on one of the symbols. The related entities are highlighted. If they are
the same entities, one of the redundant relationships can be removed.
Number of Degrees of Freedom - The number of degrees of freedom of a V-Sketch can be
determined in analysis → Element properties . It is also possible to include the number of
degrees of freedom in the tooltip.
Free dependencies within a V-sketch are displayed as points or positions if the display of
Number of degrees of freedom has been configured for the tooltip.
CAD
377

-- 391 of 622 --

1 2 3
1 	6 degrees of freedom
2 	4 degrees of freedom,
3 	0 degrees of freedom

Point-Line A point or position is arranged in such a way that it is
at the same ‘height’ with respect to the selected curve
and its imaginary extension. This height is maintained in
case of modifications.
Coincident Two points or positions are modified in such a way that
they assume and maintain a joint position. The first posi-
tion determines the location.
Coincidence X Two points or positions are arranged in such a way that
they are at the same ‘height’ with respect to the X direc-
tion (horizontal). This height is maintained in case of
modifications. The first position determines the ‘height’.
Coincidence Y Two points or positions are arranged in such a way that
they are at the same ‘height’ with respect to the vertical.
This height is maintained in case of modifications. The
first position determines the ‘height’.

Example 16. Parallel constraint between two V-sketches
Create a constraint for two V-sketches, so that two curves remain parallel to each other.
First select the curve in the first V-sketch, then select the curve in the second V-sketch.
Then specify the type of constraint. In the first V-sketch, the curve will change so that it runs
parallel to the curve selected from the second V-sketch.

14. Parametric modeling
Solids
In contrast to a static CAD model, with the parametric model a new parametric geometry
object is created for each command used, meaning that in addition to the mathematical
description the creation command, default parameters and default geometry are stored. The
new result object can now again be the default for further actions. As such, a model tree
is generated due to the fact that each geometry object is based on the default objects.
Define the chronological order and construction logic, in what way and in which order they
influence objects.
Overview of commands
Change the status of entities from static to parametric.
Context menu → Convert to parametric
Remove information on parameters of the selected entities.
Context menu → Convert to static
Roll back all entities up to selected entity.
Edit → Parametric → Roll back
Context menu → Roll back
Roll forward all entities up to selected entity.
Edit → Parametric → Roll forward
Context menu → Roll forward
Deactivate features.
Context menu → Deactivate
Reactivate selected deactivated features.
Context menu → Activate
Assign Boolean tag to a feature.
Context menu → Assign variable
No longer assign a variable to a parametric feature.
Context menu → Deassign variable
Update a parametric model.
Edit → Parametric → Rebuild parametric model
Switch automatic rebuild on and off after a change in the parametric design.
Edit → Parametric → Automatic rebuild on / off
Difference between direct modeling and parametric modeling
There are two basic procedures:
CAD
388

-- 402 of 622 --

• Case A: Import of an existing CAD model from third party CAD software
Direct modeling is required as a functionality, as a static CAD model is always present
following the import. Changes can be made to the model during work preparation, which
do not interfere with the “basic structure” of the construction. These include inserting
fillets, deleting fillets or pockets.
Converting an imported model to a parametric model enables new features to subse-
quently be created. The basic (imported) entities do however remain static. After switch-
ing to parametric modelling, direct modelling can no longer be performed. A mixed situa-
tion occurs. This mixed situation is supported.
Parametric reconstruction of removed elements
Clean up CAD model
Insert new parametric elements
Importing Static model
Direct modelling
Activate parametric modeling
Other parametric solids and features
Static solids with parametric features
NOTICE
Direct modeling is not possible on a parametric CAD model!
• Case B: Reconstruction of a CAD model in
hyperMILL®
In the case of a reconstruction, the methods of parametric modeling can be worked with
from the offset. A basic change to the construction can therefore also be subsequently
introduced. However, information on parameters can neither be exported nor imported. It
can only be based within CAD software.
Limitations with direct modeling
In the case of direct modeling, there are a number of limitations which cannot be overcome
based on the methodology used. A significant limitation is that faces and edges within a
solid, which are lost by a change, cannot be restored by a further change.
One ‘contact’ is always required for the ‘Re-trim’ principle. A section curve must therefore
always be found. Consequently, the basic structure of the model can no longer be changed
by direct modeling.
CAD
389

-- 403 of 622 --

Figure 2. Limitations with direct modeling - example
Starting with parametric modeling
Activate the Enable parametric modeling option in File → Options → Solid editing options. If
parametric modeling is activated, the menu bar will be shown in orange.
The option state is not saved in the *.hmc document. The parametric feature creation and
parametric commands are deactivated if the option is disabled. It is also possible to activate
parameters in existing static documents. A parametric feature cannot be deleted if the
option is disabled. It is also not possible to perform a Roll back / Roll forward if the option is
deactivated.
Icon in the upper toolbar (state for off and on)
IMPORTANT
For each parametric modeling a V-sketch is first of all required, on which
further modeling steps can be based.

hyperMILL® jobs are accordingly marked (the right lower
tick becomes a question mark ).

Roll back / roll forward
Move forwards or backwards in the model tree. This takes place automatically if an entity
in the construction history is changed with Change parameters. If the change has been
completed, the last state is automatically restored.
NOTICE
The Roll back / roll forward of the modeling step sequence in the model
structure is not Undo / Redo. The sequence is also saved in *.hmc, for
example.
Figure 3. Roll back and roll forward in comparison to the procedure for direct modeling
A B
CAD
391

-- 405 of 622 --

A 	Automatic Roll back and Roll forward during parametric modeling:
A Roll back is automatically executed up to the point when the selected entity was created. After the large radius is changed, all
of the following steps are re-applied.
B 	Procedure for direct modeling:
First of all, all the fillets of the two edges need to be removed before the large radius can then be changed by direct modeling.
The fillet steps must then be executed once again.
NOTICE
Always model fillets at the end! Firstly create the large fillets, then the
small fillets.
If necessary, the Roll back and Roll forwardcommands can be used to manually return to
a certain point in the model tree in order to determine the state of the affected entity at this
time or to export this state. Whereby the subsequent modeling steps are discarded.

Control automatic rebuild after changes in the parametric design as required. For example,

15. Solids, features and meshes
Direct modeling Only select the faces or features of a solid that are to be modified and change the values
for position and shape specifically without knowing all of the other values. Changes can
be made interactively by dragging the handles and the manipulator. The shape of the
selected entities and / or all other faces and features that are linked to the selected entities
is adjusted automatically.
If faces become or remain a part of a solid, the faces will be highlighted (in red) in the
preview.
NOTICE
Direct modeling is not possible on a parametric CAD model!
The procedure follows these principles:
Use direct modeling for features and faces in solids that were modelled directly in the
software. Direct modeling also works for a feature within a feature, such as a fillet in a slot
A 	.
A
Direct modeling can also be used for imported or static (with no construction history) solids
B 	. In this case, all faces, such as fillets, must be removed beforehand in such a way
that the next accurate trim edge can be found automatically during the calculation. The
calculation cannot be performed without a trim edge.
B
When direct modeling is used with features that are modelled directly in the software,
existing fillet features and chamfer features within a feature are not modified as well. They
are generated automatically at the new position C 	.
CAD
399

-- 413 of 622 --

C
A feature can be modified via direct modeling or using the “Change parameters” command.
After direct modeling, the feature is broken up.
Direct modeling can only be used on solids that are open or closed.
Various options are available for direct modeling. These include:
Delete a CAD feature D 	or delete faces (no CAD feature) in the shape of a chamfer or fillet
from a solid after selecting the entities with the DEL key. The solid is closed automatically.
D
Modify features, for example, using ‘Edit > Move / copy’ E 	.
E
Modify faces F 	within a solid, for example, using ‘Edit > Move / copy’.
F
Mirror features using ‘Edit > Mirror’ G 	.

rotation curve that are still open are closed by faces perpendicular to the rotation axis.
The Multishell solid option allows spatially separated subareas for a solid. Otherwise, indi-
vidual solids are created in correlation to the number of separate subareas.
Note: If curves within V-sketches or parametrically created faces are preselected, the option
is automatically activated. It cannot be deactivated and is greyed out. The option can be
switched when there is no preselection or if the preselection includes static entities.
With Invert 5 	, the slot remains as a solid, and the rest of the initial solid is discarded.

Pattern
Multiply existing CAD features or solids to form a determining pattern
and modify the pattern.
Features → Pattern
Solids
Select
Entities: Select solid or features 3 	. The number of selected entities is displayed.
Fast mode: Choose between a quickly generated pattern feature and a subsequently
changeable pattern feature:
• Static modeling: Do not use Fast mode if one of the copies or the base feature could be
disconnected, moved or redefined. Even if copies or the base feature itself is removed
from the pattern feature, the pattern feature can still be changed. Use Fast mode when
many copies are created within the pattern feature or when no changes need to be made
to the initially selected entity. The mode is faster, but a change would cause the pattern
to break.
• Parametric modeling: A pattern feature is always calculated in Fast mode. The option is
greyed out and inactive in the user interface.

Modify → Invert mesh orientation
Solids
Invert the normals of triangles in meshes. The normals are assumed to be in the middle of a
triangle. The color of the rear side appears darker.
Automatic orientation: All triangles of each possible cluster in the mesh are realigned
coherently. In each instance, the orientation is aligned based on the first triangle of each
cluster 1 	. If this first triangle normal faces inwards, all other triangles will then face inwards.
And if it faces outwards, then all remaining triangles will also face outwards.

Smooth meshes
Remove incorrect outliers with minimum distortion of the original sur-
face.
Modify → Smooth meshes
Solids
Remove incorrect outliers with minimum distortion of the original surface. The smoothing
algorithm is newly applied for each iteration and new positions for all nodes are calculated.
The number of triangles remains the same. For smoothing, a distance vector between two
nodes is calculated in each case and projected on an underlying plane. If the length of the
projection is greater than the maximum distance allowed, the distance between the nodes is
reduced to the length of the projection.
Max. distance allowed: The newly calculated nodes are compared with the original nodes.
If the distance is greater than the maximum distance allowed, it is reduced to ensure the
maximum distance is adhered to.
Iterations: Enter the number of repetitions of the calculation for the approximation to the
desired result.
Select
Mesh: Select an entity 1 	. If Keep attributes has not been selected, the attributes that are
active for new entities will be used. If Keep original is selected, the original entities are
retained.

Certa Systems → QMeasure
A tool electrode is divided into sections. It consists of erosion faces H 	with the contact
faces I 	as the faces at the highest point of the erosion face area. There are three exten-
sions E 	F 	G 	of the electrode shaft, the reduction C 	with the reduction top face D 	, the
block B 	as the remainder of the raw material J 	and the electrode holder A 	.
The electrode can optionally be fitted with a joint M 	to create a side electrode, which makes
it possible to reach otherwise inaccessible areas of the geometry.
An electrode can be moved and rotated along an eroding path. The eroding path can be
spatial.
The head of an eroding machine can be used for the collision check. The movement of the
electrode can be simulated in an eroding machine model and checked for collisions.

-- 458 of 622 --

IMPORTANT
The generation of an electrode is based on a 1:1 scaling of the model
geometry. It is not possible to change the scaling. All values, such as
the start and end points as well as the dimensions in the electrode
documentation, are therefore generated.
An electrode is non-parametric and non-associative to the information
about the electrode that is carried in TAGs. If changes are made to the
geometry of an electrode after it has been generated, this can lead to
incorrect tag values and must be checked and corrected accordingly.
The following changes should no longer be made after the first electrode
generation, as these changes no longer have any influence on existing
values and therefore imply incorrect values.
• Transformation of the EDM workplane
• Transformation of the electrode workplane
• Transformation of all solids or faces within the electrode group
• Transformation of the workpiece
Prepare the model geometry to derive electrodes. The undersizes (spark gap) are created
by milling. The erosion faces thereby correspond to the model geometry. Features such
as holes and breakthroughs inside a
Domain (with a single continuous face boundary)
are optionally closed during derivation, meaning they are not included when generating
the electrode geometry 1 	. Exclude other breakthroughs (that is, those with multiple face
boundaries) manually using auxiliary faces 2 	.

Milling
Electrodes can be constructed from volume and face models. The solid, open solid and
face entities plus models mixed from these entities are permitted as the reference geome-
try. Determine an area for an electrode by selecting faces of the model geometry 3 	, by
limiting with a 2D chain of curves 4 	or manually prepare the electrode geometry directly 5 	.
Imported CAD geometry data often does not have the quality required to create an elec-
trode. Despite undercut and missing faces, gaps between faces, and overlapping faces,
the software allows electrodes to be created on the basis of such CAD geometry data. A
warning is only issued if the electrode geometry does not specify a closed shape. It is up to
the user to assess whether such a situation will have a negative impact on the generation,
NC programming, and production of the electrode, and whether it needs to be corrected in
advance.
Determine areas to be eroded with the help of the Analysis → Shape spherical command.

The length of the raw material in Z is calculated based on the size of the erosion faces, the
shaft length with any correction necessitated by collision avoidance and the safety distance
11 	, the reduction, the block and the clamping depth of the raw material in the electrode
holder.
The dimensions of the raw material in X and Y are influenced by the size of the erosion
faces, optionally optimized by rotating the electrode – using Optimized C angle 12 	for the
best fit. The width of the spark gap, a negative dimension, is not taken into consideration.
Based on these dimensions, a raw material that is suitable for X and Y or the diameter is
searched for in the *.holders.xml electrode holder library and then the appropriate Z length
of this raw material is selected. This results in the assigned electrode holder. If there is no
sufficient Z length, the system moves on to the next larger raw material. The necessary
shaft length for the safety distance is calculated again for each search step. The overhang
of the raw material can be controlled using the Raw material → Z length option 10 	.

13
The target position for the erosion process is calculated.
An eroding path can be made visible and changed to influence the movement of the elec-
trode through the workpiece and the erosion movement during the erosion process. The
initial situation is the situation after the generation of an electrode in which an electrode is
moved back and forth from three positions on the same eroding path. This eroding path can
be changed so that an electrode can also be moved back and forth on the same eroding
path from more than three positions or moved from a start position to a target position. The
electrode can be rotated selectively or continuously.
Dimensioning and TAGs can be used to document the values in the electrode overview.

for the milling NC programming.

Update 3.
The electrode properties are always saved using the commands Save,
Save as and Save selection. That being said, the electrode properties
are only loaded using the commands Open, New, and New from tem-
plate, and not using the Merge command or when providing them inter-
nally; for example, holder data or machine data. The reason for this
is that the electrode options are a single instance that apply to all
documents. This means that when a document is loaded, the existing
electrode parameters are overwritten. If the default electrode parameters
are required, the electrode properties must be reset or reloaded in the
Electrode options dialogue. Put briefly, the last one wins.

Extensions
Min. tangent extension: Select the minimum length of a tangent extension 1 	at the edges
of the erosion faces. A tangent extension is not possible if the geometry crosses over
from the erosion area into the remaining workpiece geometry as a curve. No extension is
generated if 0 is specified.
Min. linear extension: Select the minimum length of a linear extension 2 	at the edges of
the erosion faces or the tangential extension. Use collision avoidance and a distance for
collision avoidance to increase the length. No extension is generated if 0 is specified.
Length for stabilization extension: To stabilize an electrode, add a stabilization extension 4
by entering a length. No extension is generated if 0 is specified.
Angle for stabilization extension: Enter an angle for the stabilization extension.
Min. block height: Enter the minimum length of the block 3 	.
The length of the block can change by adjusting to the rounded position of the electrode
holder – if a min. block height not equal to 0 is defined.
If the block height is 0, the correction for rounding the target position is applied to the linear
extension. The remaining block height or clamping depth for clamping in the electrode hold-
er remains the same. The reduction is automatically deactivated. If the reference position
of the electrode is set to the reduction bottom edge or the electrode holder, the reference
position is automatically corrected to the coupling of the electrode holder to the machine
axis if the block length is changed to 0.
The length may be increased by reducing the remaining material at the tip of the electrode.
1	2	3
4
Minimum block offset: Necessary minimum offset to be maintained at the transition between
electrode geometry and electrode block or side electrode joint 5 	.

A rotational electrode cannot be created if a side electrode is to be created.
Rotational
Rotate electrodes to combine roughing and finishing electrodes for an electrode holder.
Use the Rotational electrodes option to switch from single electrode A 	to rotational elec-
trode B 	. Use the Number of copies 3 	option to specify how many electrodes are to be
created, for example, two for a roughing and a finishing electrode.
The Angle of copies 2 	option defines by how much the electrodes continue to be rotated,
for example, by 180° if the roughing and the finishing electrode are to lie opposite each
other. The value for rotational electrode is permitted in the minus range, that is for clockwise
rotation.
The Distance to the rotation axis 1 	option specifies the distance between the individual
electrodes. The Direction of the rotation axis 4 	option specifies whether the distance refers
to the X or Y axis of the reference system. Collision warnings for collisions with the work-
piece are output on the Info tab.

Target position
Target position: The target position in relation to the electrode reference position.
CAD

-- 470 of 622 --

Automatic target position: Automatically calculate the target position.
To round: Correct the position of an electrode holder, together with the block and the
reduction, for the erosion geometry, so as to generate a ‘round’ value for the target position
X, Y, Z. For a ‘grid width’ of 1, this will result in a position of 30 instead of 30.234, for
example 2 	.
Select a value between 0 and 1 for this grid of possible positions 3 	. The target position is
not changed if 0 is entered 1 	.
The correction takes place within the scope defined by the block size in X and Y and the
size of the raw material in Z. The height of the block changes. Correction is not possible if
the electrode reference position is on the top face or the electrode tip.
Coordinates: Shows the found coordinates of the target position in relation to the electrode
reference position. If the Automatic target position option is switched off, change the target
position of the electrode to optimize the position. The position of the holder can be moved
freely and is not limited to the scope defined by the block size in X and Y and the size of the
raw material in Z.
